<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the ordinary Iitaka subadditivity conjecture for surjective projective morphisms with connected fibers between smooth connected projective varieties over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. If <i>F</i> is the geometric generic fiber of $`f:X\to Z`$, then $`\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z)`$.

- **claim-ordinary-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-ordinary-subadditivity)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a surjective projective morphism with connected fibres between smooth connected projective $k$-varieties. If $F$ is its geometric generic fibre, then $$\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z).$$
  - Explanation: Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, the Kodaira dimension of the source of a connected-fibre projective fibration is at least the sum of those of the geometric generic fibre and the base.
- **claim-adjoint-positivity-projective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-p-observation)): Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety, let $U\subset Y$ be a dense Zariski open subset, and let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation of Hodge structure on $U$ whose entire highest nonzero Hodge filtration step is a line. Let $M\in\operatorname{Pic}(Y)\otimes\mathbf Q$. Assume that, after a smooth projective birational modification of $Y$ if necessary, there are a smooth projective alteration $\tau:\widehat Y\to Y$ and a rational number $c>0$ such that the pulled-back variation has unipotent monodromy along a simple-normal-crossing boundary and $$\tau^*M\sim_{\mathbf Q}cJ_{\widehat Y},$$ where $J_{\widehat Y}$ is its extended highest line. After further smooth projective birational modification, there are a surjective morphism $p:Y\to S$ with connected fibres onto a smooth projective variety and a nef rational line $L$ on $S$ such that $$M\sim_{\mathbf Q}p^*L,\qquad K_S+jL\text{ is big for all sufficiently large integers }j.$$ If $S$ is a point, the conclusion is $M\sim_{\mathbf Q}0$.
  - Explanation: A rational class that is a positive multiple of the extended whole highest Hodge line, after an alteration, descends along a connected-fibre morphism to a nef line on a smooth projective base whose adjoint class is big for large multiples.
- **claim-minimal-subvariation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-minimal)): There is a unique smallest rational subvariation $V^{\min}\subset V_{\mathbf Q}$ whose Hodge filtration contains $F^mV_{\mathcal O}$. At a Hodge-generic point, its fibre is the smallest rational Hodge substructure containing the Hodge line, and that fibre is simple. The subvariation has an induced polarization and integral lattice. On a smooth compactification with unipotent boundary monodromy, its extended Hodge line agrees with that of $V$.
  - Explanation: There is a unique smallest rational subvariation containing the deepest Hodge line; its generic fibre is simple and its extended Hodge line agrees with the original one.
- **claim-normal-subgroup-trivial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-p-normality)): Let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on a smooth connected complex algebraic variety, minimal for its deepest Hodge line. In a flat trivialization on the universal cover, let $G\subset\mathrm{GL}(V)$ be connected algebraic monodromy and let $P=\operatorname{MT}(V)$ be the generic Mumford–Tate group. If $H\subset G$ is a connected normal algebraic subgroup defined over $\mathbf Q$, and $V^H_{\mathbf C}$ contains the deepest line at a Hodge-generic lifted point, then $H$ is trivial.
  - Explanation: For a variation minimal for its deepest Hodge line, a connected rational normal algebraic subgroup of monodromy whose invariants contain that line must be trivial.
- **claim-flat-automorphism-fixes-period** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-p-inertia)): Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety with a rationally polarized integral pure variation $\mathbb V$ minimal for its deepest Hodge line. Suppose that an automorphism $b$ of $U$ fixes a point $e$, and that there is a flat isomorphism of variations $$\Phi_y:V_y\longrightarrow V_{b(y)}.$$ Use the fibre $V_e$ as the flat reference space, and put $a=\Phi_e$. If, on a nonempty open subset of the universal cover, all deepest Hodge lines lie in a single eigenspace of $a$, then $a$ preserves the complete Hodge filtration at every lifted point. In particular, locally at $e$ the full lifted period maps at $y$ and $b(y)$ are equal.
  - Explanation: A flat automorphism over a fixed point that sends the deepest lines into one eigenspace preserves the whole Hodge filtration, so the full lifted periods agree locally.
- **claim-extension-positivity-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-inputs)): Under these hypotheses:* 1. *The extended Hodge filtration is by subbundles of $\overline V$. For a morphism $g:(T',D')\to(T,D)$ of smooth projective varieties with reduced simple normal crossings boundaries and $g(T'\setminus D')\subset U$, the extended Hodge line of $g^*V$ is $g^*J$. These extensions commute with direct sums and tensor products. If the monodromy logarithms of some boundary components vanish, the variation extends purely across those components, including their crossings away from the remaining boundary.* 2. *The line $J$ is nef. It is big precisely when the top Kodaira–Spencer map $$T_U\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F^mV_{\mathcal O},F^{m-1}V_{\mathcal O}/F^mV_{\mathcal O})$$ is generically injective, equivalently when $\ell$ is generically immersive.* *The first assertions are the unipotent extension and nilpotent-orbit theorems of (Schmid 1973; Cattani, Kaplan, and Schmid 1986) [[Schmid](#ref-Schmid), [CKS](#ref-CKS)]; functoriality is also recorded in (Bakker et al. 2025, Lemmas 2.16 and 5.6) [[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)]. The second assertion is (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023, Lemma 6.17) [[BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman](#ref-BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman)].
  - Explanation: Records the standard extension, functoriality and nefness facts for the extended Hodge line, and the criterion for bigness through generic injectivity of the top Kodaira-Spencer map.
- **claim-numerical-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-numerical-rank)): If $r=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}_{\mathrm{gen}}d\ell$, then $$\label{altord:h:eq-numerical-rank} \nu(J)=r.$$
  - Explanation: The numerical dimension of the extended Hodge line equals the generic differential rank of the highest-line map.
- **claim-boundary-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-boundary-line)): On a transverse coordinate chart $(u,v)$ about a general point of $E$, with $E=(u=0)$, write a generator of the lifted Hodge line as $$\label{altord:h:nilpotent-expression} e^{zN}a(u,v),\qquad z=\frac{\log u}{2\pi i},$$ where $a$ is holomorphic across $u=0$ and $a(0,v)\ne0$. After shrinking the chart there is an integer $\ell\ge0$ such that $$\label{altord:h:primitive-line} N^{\ell+1}a(0,v)=0,\qquad N^\ell a(0,v)\ne0,$$ and the line belongs to weight $w+\ell$ in the limiting mixed Hodge structure. Moreover, $$\label{altord:h:boundary-rank} \nu(J\|_E)\le \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}_{\mathrm{gen}}d\bigl(v\longmapsto[N^\ell a(0,v)]\bigr).$$ The map on the right is formed in the chosen flat coordinates. If $N=0$, take $\ell=0$; the variation then extends purely near the general point of $E$.
  - Explanation: Near a general boundary point the lifted Hodge line has a nilpotent-orbit form, and the numerical dimension of its restriction to the divisor is bounded by the rank of the limiting primitive line map.
- **claim-rank-principle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-rank-principle)): Let $B\subset\mathbf C^n$ be a polydisc and let $f:S_\epsilon\times B\to M$ be a holomorphic map to a complex manifold. Suppose $f(u,\cdot)$ converges uniformly on compact subsets of $B$, as $u\to0$ throughout the sector, to a holomorphic map $f_0$. Assume that the full generic differential rank of $f$ is at most $r$ and that $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}df_0(v_0)=r$ at some $v_0\in B$. Then, after shrinking the sector and taking neighborhoods $v_0\in B'\Subset B_0\subset B$, $$f(S_{\epsilon'}\times B')\subset f_0(B_0),$$ where the indicated local image of $f_0$ is an embedded complex submanifold of dimension $r$. In particular, every fixed closed analytic subset containing this local image also contains the nearby image of $f$.
  - Explanation: If a family of holomorphic maps has generic rank at most r and its tangential limit attains rank r, then nearby members of the family map into the limiting r-dimensional image.
- **claim-nilpotent-tangential-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit)): Let $N$ be a nilpotent endomorphism of a finite-dimensional complex vector space, and let $a(u,v)$ be holomorphic on $\Delta\times B$. Suppose, for a fixed $\ell\ge0$, that $N^{\ell+1}a(0,v)=0$ and $N^\ell a(0,v)\ne0$ throughout $B$. On a sector with the above choice of $z=\log u/(2\pi i)$, $$\label{altord:h:eq-nilpotent-limit} [e^{zN}a(u,v)]\longrightarrow[N^\ell a(0,v)]$$ normally on sufficiently small $v$-charts, with all tangential derivatives. Thus Lemma <a href="#altord:h:rank-principle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="altord:h:rank-principle">9</a> applies whenever the rank of this limit equals an upper bound for the full rank of the line map. Its nearby image then lies in $\mathbb P(\ker N)$.
  - Explanation: The projectivized nilpotent orbit converges with all tangential derivatives to the projectivized top nilpotent image, whose image lies in the projectivized kernel of the logarithm.
- **claim-rankloss** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rankloss)): For the data just specified, put $r=\nu(J)$. Then $r>0$. If $E$ is either a component of $D_N$, or a component of $D_T$ fixed pointwise by a nonidentity element of $G_f$, then $$\nu(J\|_E)<r.$$ In particular, after placing the divisorial fixed loci in $D_T$ on an equivariant smooth model, the conclusion applies to every divisorial ramification component of $T\to T/G_f$.
  - Explanation: The highest line has positive numerical dimension, and its numerical dimension strictly drops on every boundary component with nonzero monodromy logarithm and on every divisor fixed by a nonidentity finite inertia element.
- **claim-volume-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-e-volume-lemma)): Let $T$ be a smooth projective $t$-fold, $A$ a rational divisor, and $J$ a nef rational divisor with $r=\nu(J)>0$, and $D=\sum_i d_iE_i$ an effective integral divisor with smooth prime components. Suppose $$\nu(J\|_{E_i})<r\quad\text{for every }i.$$ If $A+D$ is big, then $A+jJ$ is big for every sufficiently large integer $j$.
  - Explanation: If the numerical dimension of a nef divisor drops on every component of an effective divisor, then bigness of the sum with that divisor implies bigness of the sum with large multiples of the nef divisor.
- **claim-exceptional-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-e-exceptional-pullback)): Let $\pi:T\to S$ be a surjective generically finite morphism of smooth projective varieties. Let $E\geq0$ be a rational divisor on $T$ whose components have image of codimension at least two in $S$. For a rational divisor $D$ on $S$, the divisor $\pi^*D+E$ is big if and only if $D$ is big.
  - Explanation: Adding an effective divisor whose components map to high-codimension loci does not change bigness of a pullback under a generically finite surjection.
- **claim-parabolic-canonical-bundle-formula** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-parabolic)): Let $g_0:X_0\to Y_0$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth projective varieties, with geometric generic fibre of Kodaira dimension zero. After birational modifications of its source and base there is a commutative diagram $$\begin{array}{ccc} X'&\xrightarrow{\alpha}&X_0\\ {\scriptstyle g}\downarrow&&\downarrow{\scriptstyle g_0}\\ Y&\xrightarrow{\beta}&Y_0 \end{array}$$ with $X'$ and $Y$ smooth, and rational divisors $B,M,R$ such that $$\label{altord:cb:formula} K_{X'}\sim_{\mathbf Q}g^*(K_Y+B+M)+R.$$ Here $B\geq0$, its support has simple normal crossings, $(Y,B)$ is klt, and $M$ is nef. Moreover, $$\label{altord:cb:residual} g_*\mathcal O_{X'}(\lfloor kR^+\rfloor)=\mathcal O_Y \quad(k\in\mathbf Z_{>0}), \qquad \operatorname{codim}_Y g(\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}R^-)\geq2,$$ and $R^-$ is $\alpha$-exceptional. The divisor $M$ is the trace of the moduli b-divisor, which can be assumed to descend to $Y$. On a sufficiently high generically finite base change admitting semistable reduction, its pullback is $\mathbf Q$-linearly equivalent to a positive rational multiple of the Schmid extension of the distinguished canonical Hodge eigensheaf of the canonical root cover.
  - Explanation: After birational modifications, the canonical class decomposes into a pullback of a klt boundary and a nef moduli part plus a controlled residual divisor, with the moduli part tied to a Hodge line of the root cover.
- **claim-canonical-root-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-root-cover)): Let $C$ be a smooth connected projective variety with $\kappa(C)=0$, and let $b>0$ be the least integer for which $H^0(C,bK_C)\ne0$. Choose a nonzero section $s$ of $bK_C$ and a nonzero rational canonical form $\omega$. Let $T$ be the normalization of $C$ in the field obtained by adjoining a root $$\xi^b=\frac{s}{\omega^b}.$$ Then $T\to C$ has degree $b$, and every smooth projective resolution $W$ of $T$ satisfies $$\kappa(W)=0,\qquad h^0(W,K_W)=1.$$ The unique canonical line is spanned by the form $\xi h^*\omega$, where $h:W\to C$ is the induced morphism.
  - Explanation: The least-index root cover of a Kodaira-dimension-zero variety has resolutions of Kodaira dimension zero and geometric genus one, with a distinguished spanning canonical form.
- **claim-canonical-bundle-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-reduction)): Let $f:X\to Z$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth connected projective varieties, and let its geometric generic fibre $F$ have $d=\kappa(F)\geq0$. There exist smooth projective varieties $\widetilde X,Y$, a birational morphism $\mu:\widetilde X\to X$, and algebraic fibre spaces $$\widetilde X\xrightarrow{g}Y\xrightarrow{q}Z,\qquad qg=f\mu,$$ such that $\dim Y-\dim Z=d$ and the geometric generic fibre of $g$ has Kodaira dimension zero. They admit the data $B,M,R$ of Theorem <a href="#altord:cb:parabolic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="altord:cb:parabolic">16</a>, with $R^-$ exceptional over $X$. The moduli divisor $M$ has the realization <a href="#altord:cb:hodge-realization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="altord:cb:hodge-realization">[altord:cb:hodge-realization]</a> for a polarizable rational variation whose top Hodge piece has rank one. Writing $$D=K_Y+B+M,$$ one has $$\label{altord:cb:two-comparisons} \kappa(X)\geq\kappa(Y,D), \qquad D\|_{Y_{\bar\eta}}\text{ is big},$$ where $\bar\eta$ is a geometric generic point of $Z$. The same bigness holds on very general smooth fibres of $q$.
  - Explanation: A relative Iitaka reduction yields a factorization with the canonical bundle data, such that the source Kodaira dimension bounds that of the adjoint divisor and that divisor is big on the geometric generic fibre over the base.
- **claim-section-growth-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-comparison)): For the factorization and canonical bundle data in Proposition <a href="#altord:cb:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="altord:cb:reduction">19</a>, <a href="#altord:cb:two-comparisons" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="altord:cb:two-comparisons">[altord:cb:two-comparisons]</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Section-growth comparison establishes both the Kodaira dimension inequality and bigness on the geometric generic fibre for the reduction data.
- **claim-high-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-high-model)): Suppose $Y,B,M,D$ satisfy the conclusions of Proposition <a href="#altord:cb:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="altord:cb:reduction">19</a>. Let $\nu:Y'\to Y$ be a smooth projective log resolution, and write $$K_{Y'}+B^{\mathrm{cr}}=\nu^*(K_Y+B).$$ Set $B'=(B^{\mathrm{cr}})^+$, $M'=\nu^*M$, and $D'=K_{Y'}+B'+M'$. Then $B'$ is effective, $(Y',B')$ is klt, $M'$ is nef with the same Hodge realization after pullback, and $$D'=\nu^*D+(B^{\mathrm{cr}})^-.$$ The additional divisor is effective and $\nu$-exceptional. Consequently $\kappa(Y',D')=\kappa(Y,D)$, and $D'$ is big on the geometric generic fibre over $Z$.
  - Explanation: Passing to a log resolution preserves klt singularities, nefness and Hodge realization of the moduli part, the Iitaka dimension, and generic bigness.
- **claim-log-general-type-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-a-log-general-type)): Let $u:V\to T$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibres, where $V$ is normal projective and $T$ is smooth projective. Let $\Delta\geq0$ be a $\mathbf Q$-divisor such that $(V,\Delta)$ is log canonical. If the log canonical divisor of the geometric generic fibre $G$ is big and $\kappa(T)\geq0$, then $$\kappa(V,K_V+\Delta)\geq \dim G+\kappa(T).$$
  - Explanation: For a log canonical pair whose generic fibre has big log canonical divisor, the log Kodaira dimension is at least the fibre dimension plus the Kodaira dimension of the base.
- **claim-twisted-weak-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-a-weak-positivity)): Let $u:V\to T$ be a surjective morphism from a normal projective variety to a smooth projective variety, and let $(V,\Delta)$ be an effective log canonical $\mathbf Q$-pair. If $k$ is a positive integer such that $k(K_V+\Delta)$ is Cartier, then $$u_*\mathcal O_V\bigl(nk(K_{V/T}+\Delta)\bigr)$$ is weakly positive for every positive integer $n$.
  - Explanation: Direct images of multiples of the relative log canonical class of an effective log canonical pair are weakly positive.
- **claim-period-fibre-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-a-period-fibre)): Let $p:Y\to S$ and $q:Y\to Z$ be algebraic fibre spaces between smooth projective varieties. Let $(Y,B)$ be an effective klt $\mathbf Q$-pair and let $M\sim_{\mathbf Q}p^*L$ for a $\mathbf Q$-divisor $L$ on $S$. Suppose that $\kappa(Z)\geq0$ and $$D:=K_Y+B+M$$ is big on the geometric generic fibre of $q$. Then the geometric generic fibre of $p$ has nonnegative log Kodaira dimension.
  - Explanation: Under the stated bigness on generic fibres of the base map, the geometric generic fibre of the period projection has nonnegative log Kodaira dimension.
- **claim-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-a-effectivity)): Let $p:Y\to S$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth projective varieties, and let $(Y,B)$ be an effective klt $\mathbf Q$-pair. Suppose that the geometric generic fibre has nonnegative log Kodaira dimension. For every big $\mathbf Q$-divisor $P$ on $S$, the divisor $$K_{Y/S}+B+p^*P$$ is $\mathbf Q$-linearly equivalent to an effective divisor.
  - Explanation: If the generic fibre has nonnegative log Kodaira dimension, the relative log canonical class twisted by a big divisor from the base is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective divisor.
- **claim-field-extension-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-a-field-extension)): Let $K\subset K'$ be a field extension and let $V$ be a smooth projective geometrically integral variety over $K$. The Kodaira dimension of $V$ is unchanged by extending scalars to $K'$. The same assertion holds for the geometric generic fibre of an algebraic fibre space between smooth projective geometrically integral varieties when the ground field is extended.
  - Explanation: Kodaira dimension of a smooth projective geometrically integral variety, and of the geometric generic fibre of a fibre space, is unchanged by extension of the ground field.
- **claim-integral-adjoint-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-positivity)): Let $P$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety and $D$ a reduced simple normal crossing divisor. Let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on $P\setminus D$ whose highest nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line, and let $L\in\operatorname{Pic}(P)\otimes\mathbf Q$ be its rational parabolic extension. There are a projective birational morphism $\pi:\widetilde P\to P$, with $\widetilde P$ smooth, a smooth projective variety $Z$, a surjective morphism $h:\widetilde P\to Z$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational divisor $A$ on $Z$ such that $$\pi^*L\sim_{\mathbf Q}h^*A, \qquad K_Z+aA\ \text{is big for every sufficiently large rational }a.$$ Here a divisor on a point is big. Further smooth birational modifications of the source are permitted by replacing $h$ with its composition with the modification.
  - Explanation: For the rational parabolic extension of a whole highest Hodge line, after birational modification it descends along a connected-fibre morphism to a nef divisor whose adjoint class becomes big for large multiples.
- **claim-extension-facts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-extensions)): The rational extension has the following properties.* 1. *It is nef and is compatible with positive tensor powers and pullback by morphisms of smooth projective log pairs whose interiors map to the original interior.* 2. *In the unipotent case, on each connected boundary stratum the local limiting mixed Hodge structures determine a unique weight-graded piece containing the top Hodge line. These pieces glue to a polarizable pure variation with rank-one top filtration, whose extended top line on the closure of the stratum is the restriction of $L$.* 3. *The preceding restriction statement remains valid after an adapted cover in the quasi-unipotent case. In particular, numerical dimensions of restrictions may be computed on a smooth adapted cover of the stratum.
  - Explanation: The rational extension is nef, functorial under powers and log pullbacks, and its restriction to boundary strata is the top line of a pure graded variation.
- **claim-curvature-intersections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-rank)): The numerical dimension of the rational top Hodge line equals the generic rank of its line period map: $$\nu(L)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(d\ell)_{\mathrm{gen}}.$$ In the unipotent case, if $d=\dim P$, $\omega$ is a smooth Kähler form, and $\alpha$ is the first Chern form of the Hodge metric on $L\|_{P^\circ}$, then, for $0\le k\le d$, $$\label{p1logalt:chern} \int_{P^\circ}\alpha^k\wedge\omega^{d-k} =c_1(L)^k\cdot[\omega]^{d-k}.$$ The integrals are absolutely convergent.
  - Explanation: The numerical dimension of the rational top Hodge line equals the generic rank of its line period map, and Chern form integrals compute intersection numbers without boundary loss in the unipotent case.
- **claim-integral-tensor-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-kernel)): Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety, and let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on $U$ whose highest nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line $L^\circ$. There are an integer $b>0$ and a rationally polarized integral pure variation $\mathbb V_1$ on $U$ satisfying Condition <a href="#p1logalt:faithful-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1logalt:faithful-line">[p1logalt:faithful-line]</a>, whose highest nonzero Hodge filtration piece is $(L^\circ)^{\otimes b}$. On any smooth projective simple-normal-crossing compactification of $U$, the rational parabolic highest line of $\mathbb V_1$ is the $b$th power of that of $\mathbb V$.
  - Explanation: A tensor power construction yields an integral polarized variation on which only the identity monodromy element fixes the moving highest line, with the highest line the corresponding power.
- **claim-full-period-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-descent)): After applying Lemma <a href="#p1logalt:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:kernel">31</a> and making smooth birational modifications of the projective models, the resulting variation descends over a dense open subset of a smooth projective variety $Z$. Its descended full period map is generically immersive and satisfies Condition <a href="#p1logalt:faithful-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1logalt:faithful-line">[p1logalt:faithful-line]</a>. If $A_1$ is its rational top Hodge extension, then the original extension satisfies $$b\pi^*L\sim_{\mathbf Q}h^*A_1$$ for a morphism $h:\widetilde P\to Z$ with connected fibers.
  - Explanation: After the tensor replacement and birational modifications, the variation descends to a smooth projective base with generically immersive full period map and a connected-fibre morphism relating the line classes.
- **claim-transverse-loop** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4int-transverse-loop)): Let $U=\Delta\times\Delta^{d-1}$, with coordinates $(z,s)$, and let $U^*=\Delta^*\times\Delta^{d-1}$. Suppose that a holomorphic map $\ell:\widetilde{U^*}\to\mathbb P(V)$ is equivariant for a linear transformation $T\in\operatorname{GL}(V)$ under one positive turn around $z=0$, and that $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(d\ell)\leq r$. Suppose that a holomorphic map $q:U\to\mathbb C^r$ has tangential differential of rank $r$ at a point of $z=0$, and that on $U^*$ its lift satisfies $\ker(d\ell)\subseteq\ker(dq)$. Then $T$ fixes every line in the image of $\ell$ on a nonempty open subset. If $\ell$ is the restriction of a line period map on a connected universal cover, $T$ fixes that entire line image. This includes $r=0$, when $q$ is the map to a point.
  - Explanation: If the kernel of the line differential lies in the kernel of a rank-full map along a boundary, a transverse loop forces the monodromy transformation to fix the moving line image.
- **claim-boundary-rank-drop** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-boundary-drop)): Let $Z$ be smooth projective, let $D$ be a reduced simple normal crossing divisor, and let a polarizable integral pure variation on $Z\setminus D$ have a top Hodge line satisfying Condition <a href="#p1logalt:faithful-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1logalt:faithful-line">[p1logalt:faithful-line]</a>. Write $A_1$ for its rational extension and $r=\nu(A_1)$. If $D_i$ is a component whose transverse monodromy is not the identity, then $$\nu(A_1\|_{D_i})<r.$$
  - Explanation: For a line satisfying the faithfulness condition, nonidentity transverse monodromy along a boundary component strictly lowers the numerical dimension of the restricted extension.
- **claim-boundary-subtraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-volume)): Let $Z$ be smooth projective of positive dimension $d$, let $A_1$ be a nef rational divisor, and let $J=\sum_iD_i$ be reduced with smooth components. Suppose $K_Z+J$ is big and $$\nu(A_1\|_{D_i})<r:=\nu(A_1)\quad\text{for every }i.$$ Then $K_Z+aA_1$ is big for every sufficiently large rational $a$.
  - Explanation: Strict numerical dimension loss on every boundary component together with bigness of the log canonical class gives bigness of the adjoint class for large multiples of the nef divisor.
- **claim-chern-closed-test-forms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1varhd-curvature)): Let $A$ be the rational parabolic highest line of an integral polarized pure variation on the complement of a reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor in a smooth projective variety $R$. Then $$\label{p1varhd:numrank} \nu(A)=\text{generic differential rank of its projective line map}$$ provided $A$ is nef. In the unipotent case, if $\alpha$ is the Chern form of its Hodge metric on $R\setminus D_R$, then for every $0\leq j\leq n=\dim R$ and every smooth closed form $\gamma$ of type $(n-j,n-j)$, the integral $\int_{R\setminus D_R}\alpha^j\wedge\gamma$ is absolutely convergent and equals $c_1(A)^j\cdot[\gamma]$.
  - Explanation: The numerical dimension equals the generic rank of the line map, and Chern form powers integrate against closed test forms to intersection numbers with no boundary contribution.
- **claim-thom-form-boundary-drop** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1varhd-boundary)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#p1varhd:curvature" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1varhd:curvature">37</a>, suppose that monodromy is faithful on the moving highest line. If $D_i$ is a boundary component with nonidentity transverse monodromy, then $$\label{p1varhd:strictdrop} \nu(A\|_{D_i})<\nu(A).$$
  - Explanation: With faithful monodromy on the moving highest line, a boundary component with nonidentity transverse monodromy has strictly smaller numerical dimension, proved via Thom forms.
- **claim-remark-scaling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-h-scaling)): All numerical-rank statements are unchanged on replacing a Hodge line by a positive rational multiple of its class in $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(T)\otimes\mathbf Q$. The local statements also apply with $N=0$, when the line map extends holomorphically across the divisor.
  - Explanation: Numerical-rank statements are insensitive to replacing a Hodge line by a positive rational multiple of its class, and the local statements also cover the case of a vanishing monodromy logarithm.
- **claim-remark-no-abundance-moduli** (theorem, [source](#stmt-altord-cb-no-abundance)): No abundance assertion for $M$ is part of the input. The stronger conclusion of (Ambro 2005, Theorem 3.3) [[AmbroModuli](#ref-AmbroModuli)] assumes effectivity of the boundary on the geometric generic fibre, an assumption which need not hold for $-R_C$. The b-nefness and Hodge identification used above instead come from the results of (Ambro 2004) [[AmbroBoundary](#ref-AmbroBoundary)] just cited. In particular, no good minimal model of $C$ is required. For the Hodge-theoretic formulation with sub-pairs, see also (Bakker et al. 2025, Definition 6.18, Construction-Definition 6.23, Remark 6.25, and Theorem 6.28) [[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)].
  - Explanation: The argument does not take abundance of the moduli divisor as an input, since the stronger known conclusion needs an effectivity assumption on the generic fibre boundary that can fail here.
- **claim-remark-no-abundance-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logalt-no-abundance)): The construction permits $\nu(A)<\dim Z$. In particular, the algebraic map $h$ is obtained from the full period map of an integral tensor replacement; its fibers are not asserted to be the leaves of the line period map. Neither abundance nor semiampleness of $A$ occurs in the argument.
  - Explanation: The construction allows the numerical dimension of the descended nef divisor to be smaller than the dimension of its base, and uses neither abundance nor semiampleness.
- **claim-remark-further-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1varhd-further-models)): The adjoint conclusion is preserved under further smooth projective birational modification $\pi:R'\to R$, together with a compatible modification of the source. The parabolic pullback property preserves the line-bundle identity and nefness, while $$K_{R'}+b\pi^*A =\pi^*(K_R+bA)+E_\pi,\qquad E_\pi\geq0,$$ preserves bigness. The calculations here concern the whole rank-one highest step of a polarizable integral pure variation on a projective pair; they assert no equality between highest-line rank, full-period rank, and whole-fiber birational variation.
  - Explanation: The adjoint conclusion survives further smooth projective birational modification of the base with a compatible modification of the source, because the canonical class changes by an effective divisor.

          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:adjoint\" label=\"thm:adjoint\"></span>Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety, let $U\\subset Y$ be a dense Zariski open subset, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation of Hodge structure on $U$ whose entire highest nonzero Hodge filtration step is a line.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-minimal",
        "exact_quote": "There is a unique smallest rational subvariation $V^{\\min}\\subset V_{\\mathbf Q}$ whose Hodge filtration contains $F^mV_{\\mathcal O}$. At a Hodge-generic point, its fibre is the smallest rational Hodge substructure containing the Hodge line, and that fibre is simple. The subvariation has an induced polarization and integral lattice. On a smooth compactification with unipotent boundary monodromy, its extended Hodge line agrees with that of $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-minimal",

          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-p-normality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on a smooth connected complex algebraic variety, minimal for its deepest Hodge line. In a flat trivialization on the universal cover, let $G\\subset\\mathrm{GL}(V)$ be connected algebraic monodromy and let $P=\\operatorname{MT}(V)$ be the generic Mumford–Tate group. If $H\\subset G$ is a connected normal algebraic subgroup defined over $\\mathbf Q$, and $V^H_{\\mathbf C}$ contains the deepest line at a Hodge-generic lifted point, then $H$ is trivial.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-p-normality",

          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-p-inertia",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety with a rationally polarized integral pure variation $\\mathbb V$ minimal for its deepest Hodge line. Suppose that an automorphism $b$ of $U$ fixes a point $e$, and that there is a flat isomorphism of variations $$\\Phi_y:V_y\\longrightarrow V_{b(y)}.$$ Use the fibre $V_e$ as the flat reference space, and put $a=\\Phi_e$. If, on a nonempty open subset of the universal cover, all deepest Hodge lines lie in a single eigenspace of $a$, then $a$ preserves the complete Hodge filtration at every lifted point. In particular, locally at $e$ the full lifted period maps at $y$ and $b(y)$ are equal.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-p-inertia",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety with a rationally polarized integral pure variation $\\mathbb V$ minimal for its deepest Hodge line. If, on a nonempty open subset of the universal cover, all deepest Hodge lines lie in a single eigenspace of $a$, then $a$ preserves the complete Hodge filtration at every lifted point. In particular, locally at $e$ the full lifted period maps at $y$ and $b(y)$ are equal.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Under these hypotheses:*\n\n1.  *The extended Hodge filtration is by subbundles of $\\overline V$. For a morphism $g:(T',D')\\to(T,D)$ of smooth projective varieties with reduced simple normal crossings boundaries and $g(T'\\setminus D')\\subset U$, the extended Hodge line of $g^*V$ is $g^*J$. These extensions commute with direct sums and tensor products. If the monodromy logarithms of some boundary components vanish, the variation extends purely across those components, including their crossings away from the remaining boundary.*\n\n2.  *The line $J$ is nef. It is big precisely when the top Kodaira–Spencer map $$T_U\\longrightarrow\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(F^mV_{\\mathcal O},F^{m-1}V_{\\mathcal O}/F^mV_{\\mathcal O})$$ is generically injective, equivalently when $\\ell$ is generically immersive.*\n\n*The first assertions are the unipotent extension and nilpotent-orbit theorems of (Schmid 1973; Cattani, Kaplan, and Schmid 1986) [[Schmid](#ref-Schmid), [CKS](#ref-CKS)]; functoriality is also recorded in (Bakker et al. 2025, Lemmas 2.16 and 5.6) [[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)]. The second assertion is (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023, Lemma 6.17) [[BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman](#ref-BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman)].",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "Under these hypotheses:*\n\n1.  *The extended Hodge filtration is by subbundles of $\\overline V$. These extensions commute with direct sums and tensor products. If the monodromy logarithms of some boundary components vanish, the variation extends purely across those components, including their crossings away from the remaining boundary.*\n\n2.  *The line $J$ is nef. It is big precisely when the top Kodaira–Spencer map $$T_U\\longrightarrow\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(F^mV_{\\mathcal O},F^{m-1}V_{\\mathcal O}/F^mV_{\\mathcal O})$$ is generically injective, equivalently when $\\ell$ is generically immersive.*\n\n*The first assertions are the unipotent extension and nilpotent-orbit theorems of (Schmid 1973; Cattani, Kaplan, and Schmid 1986) [[Schmid](#ref-Schmid), [CKS](#ref-CKS)]; functoriality is also recorded in (Bakker et al. 2025, Lemmas 2.16 and 5.6) [[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)]. The second assertion is (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023, Lemma 6.17) [[BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman](#ref-BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman)].",

        "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-boundary-line",
        "exact_quote": "On a transverse coordinate chart $(u,v)$ about a general point of $E$, with $E=(u=0)$, write a generator of the lifted Hodge line as $$\\label{altord:h:nilpotent-expression}\n e^{zN}a(u,v),\\qquad z=\\frac{\\log u}{2\\pi i},$$ where $a$ is holomorphic across $u=0$ and $a(0,v)\\ne0$. After shrinking the chart there is an integer $\\ell\\ge0$ such that $$\\label{altord:h:primitive-line}\n N^{\\ell+1}a(0,v)=0,\\qquad N^\\ell a(0,v)\\ne0,$$ and the line belongs to weight $w+\\ell$ in the limiting mixed Hodge structure. Moreover, $$\\label{altord:h:boundary-rank}\n \\nu(J|_E)\\le\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}_{\\mathrm{gen}}d\\bigl(v\\longmapsto[N^\\ell a(0,v)]\\bigr).$$ The map on the right is formed in the chosen flat coordinates. If $N=0$, take $\\ell=0$; the variation then extends purely near the general point of $E$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-rank-principle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subset\\mathbf C^n$ be a polydisc and let $f:S_\\epsilon\\times B\\to M$ be a holomorphic map to a complex manifold. Suppose $f(u,\\cdot)$ converges uniformly on compact subsets of $B$, as $u\\to0$ throughout the sector, to a holomorphic map $f_0$. Assume that the full generic differential rank of $f$ is at most $r$ and that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}df_0(v_0)=r$ at some $v_0\\in B$. Then, after shrinking the sector and taking neighborhoods $v_0\\in B'\\Subset B_0\\subset B$, $$f(S_{\\epsilon'}\\times B')\\subset f_0(B_0),$$ where the indicated local image of $f_0$ is an embedded complex submanifold of dimension $r$. In particular, every fixed closed analytic subset containing this local image also contains the nearby image of $f$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a nilpotent endomorphism of a finite-dimensional complex vector space, and let $a(u,v)$ be holomorphic on $\\Delta\\times B$. Suppose, for a fixed $\\ell\\ge0$, that $N^{\\ell+1}a(0,v)=0$ and $N^\\ell a(0,v)\\ne0$ throughout $B$. On a sector with the above choice of $z=\\log u/(2\\pi i)$, $$\\label{altord:h:eq-nilpotent-limit}\n [e^{zN}a(u,v)]\\longrightarrow[N^\\ell a(0,v)]$$ normally on sufficiently small $v$-charts, with all tangential derivatives. Thus Lemma <a href=\"#altord:h:rank-principle\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"altord:h:rank-principle\">9</a> applies whenever the rank of this limit equals an upper bound for the full rank of the line map. Its nearby image then lies in $\\mathbb P(\\ker N)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "For the data just specified, put $r=\\nu(J)$. Then $r>0$.         "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth projective $t$-fold, $A$ a rational divisor, and $J$ a nef rational divisor with $r=\\nu(J)>0$, and $D=\\sum_i d_iE_i$ an effective integral divisor with smooth prime components.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-parabolic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g_0:X_0\\to Y_0$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth projective varieties, with geometric generic fibre of Kodaira dimension zero. After birational modifications of its source and base there is a commutative diagram $$\\begin{array}{ccc}\n X'&\\xrightarrow{\\alpha}&X_0\\\\\n {\\scriptstyle g}\\downarrow&&\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle g_0}\\\\\n Y&\\xrightarrow{\\beta}&Y_0\n\\end{array}$$ with $X'$ and $Y$ smooth, and rational divisors $B,M,R$ such that $$\\label{altord:cb:formula}\n K_{X'}\\sim_{\\mathbf Q}g^*(K_Y+B+M)+R.$$ Here $B\\geq0$, its support has simple normal crossings, $(Y,B)$ is klt, and $M$ is nef. Moreover, $$\\label{altord:cb:residual}\n g_*\\mathcal O_{X'}(\\lfloor kR^+\\rfloor)=\\mathcal O_Y\n \\quad(k\\in\\mathbf Z_{>0}),\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{codim}_Y g(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}R^-)\\geq2,$$ and $R^-$ is $\\alpha$-exceptional. The divisor $M$ is the trace of the moduli b-divisor, which can be assumed to descend to $Y$. On a sufficiently high generically finite base change admitting semistable reduction, its pullback is $\\mathbf Q$-linearly equivalent to a positive rational multiple of the Schmid extension of the distinguished canonical Hodge eigensheaf of the canonical root cover.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-parabolic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $g_0:X_0\\to Y_0$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth projective varieties, with geometric generic fibre of Kodaira dimension zero. Moreover, $$\\label{altord:cb:residual}\n g_*\\mathcal O_{X'}(\\lfloor kR^+\\rfloor)=\\mathcal O_Y\n \\quad(k\\in\\mathbf Z_{>0}),\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{codim}_Y g(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}R^-)\\geq2,$$ and $R^-$ is $\\alpha$-exceptional. The divisor $M$ is the trace of the moduli b-divisor, which can be assumed to descend to $Y$. On a sufficiently high generically finite base change admitting semistable reduction, its pullback is $\\mathbf Q$-linearly equivalent to a positive rational multiple of the Schmid extension of the distinguished canonical Hodge eigensheaf of the canonical root cover.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.4"

The divisor $M$ is the trace of the moduli b-divisor, which can be assumed to descend to $Y$. On a sufficiently high generically finite base change admitting semistable reduction, its pullback is $\\mathbf Q$-linearly equivalent to a positive rational multiple of the Schmid extension of the distinguished canonical Hodge eigensheaf of the canonical root cover.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-root-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth connected projective variety with $\\kappa(C)=0$, and let $b>0$ be the least integer for which $H^0(C,bK_C)\\ne0$. Choose a nonzero section $s$ of $bK_C$ and a nonzero rational canonical form $\\omega$. Let $T$ be the normalization of $C$ in the field obtained by adjoining a root $$\\xi^b=\\frac{s}{\\omega^b}.$$ Then $T\\to C$ has degree $b$, and every smooth projective resolution $W$ of $T$ satisfies $$\\kappa(W)=0,\\qquad h^0(W,K_W)=1.$$ The unique canonical line is spanned by the form $\\xi h^*\\omega$, where $h:W\\to C$ is the induced morphism.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-root-cover",

          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth connected projective varieties, and let its geometric generic fibre $F$ have $d=\\kappa(F)\\geq0$. There exist smooth projective varieties $\\widetilde X,Y$, a birational morphism $\\mu:\\widetilde X\\to X$, and algebraic fibre spaces $$\\widetilde X\\xrightarrow{g}Y\\xrightarrow{q}Z,\\qquad\n qg=f\\mu,$$ such that $\\dim Y-\\dim Z=d$ and the geometric generic fibre of $g$ has Kodaira dimension zero. They admit the data $B,M,R$ of Theorem <a href=\"#altord:cb:parabolic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:parabolic\">16</a>, with $R^-$ exceptional over $X$. The moduli divisor $M$ has the realization <a href=\"#altord:cb:hodge-realization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:hodge-realization\">[altord:cb:hodge-realization]</a> for a polarizable rational variation whose top Hodge piece has rank one. Writing $$D=K_Y+B+M,$$ one has $$\\label{altord:cb:two-comparisons}\n \\kappa(X)\\geq\\kappa(Y,D),\n \\qquad\n D|_{Y_{\\bar\\eta}}\\text{ is big},$$ where $\\bar\\eta$ is a geometric generic point of $Z$. The same bigness holds on very general smooth fibres of $q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth connected projective varieties, and let its geometric generic fibre $F$ have $d=\\kappa(F)\\geq0$. There exist smooth projective varieties $\\widetilde X,Y$, a birational morphism $\\mu:\\widetilde X\\to X$, and algebraic fibre spaces $$\\widetilde X\\xrightarrow{g}Y\\xrightarrow{q}Z,\\qquad\n qg=f\\mu,$$ such that $\\dim Y-\\dim Z=d$ and the geometric generic fibre of $g$ has Kodaira dimension zero. They admit the data $B,M,R$ of Theorem <a href=\"#altord:cb:parabolic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:parabolic\">16</a>, with $R^-$ exceptional over $X$. The moduli divisor $M$ has the realization <a href=\"#altord:cb:hodge-realization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:hodge-realization\">[altord:cb:hodge-realization]</a> for a polarizable rational variation whose top Hodge piece has rank one. Writing $$D=K_Y+B+M,$$ one has $$\\label{altord:cb:two-comparisons}\n \\kappa(X)\\geq\\kappa(Y,D),\n \\qquad\n D|_{Y_{\\bar\\eta}}\\text{ is big},$$ where $\\bar\\eta$ is a geometric generic point of $Z$. The same bigness holds on very general smooth fibres of $q$.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be an algebraic fibre space between smooth connected projective varieties, and let its geometric generic fibre $F$ have $d=\\kappa(F)\\geq0$. There exist smooth projective varieties $\\widetilde X,Y$, a birational morphism $\\mu:\\widetilde X\\to X$, and algebraic fibre spaces $$\\widetilde X\\xrightarrow{g}Y\\xrightarrow{q}Z,\\qquad\n qg=f\\mu,$$ such that $\\dim Y-\\dim Z=d$ and the geometric generic fibre of $g$ has Kodaira dimension zero. They admit the data $B,M,R$ of Theorem <a href=\"#altord:cb:parabolic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:parabolic\">16</a>, with $R^-$ exceptional over $X$. The moduli divisor $M$ has the realization <a href=\"#altord:cb:hodge-realization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:hodge-realization\">[altord:cb:hodge-realization]</a> for a polarizable rational variation whose top Hodge piece has rank one. Writing $$D=K_Y+B+M,$$ one has $$\\label{altord:cb:two-comparisons}\n \\kappa(X)\\geq\\kappa(Y,D),\n \\qquad\n D|_{Y_{\\bar\\eta}}\\text{ is big},$$ where $\\bar\\eta$ is a geometric generic point of $Z$. The same bigness holds on very general smooth fibres of $q$.",
      "explanation": "A relative Iitaka reduction yields a factorization with the canonical bundle data, such that the source Kodaira dimension bounds that of the adjoint divisor and that divisor is big on the geometric generic fibre over the base.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-cb-high-model",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $Y,B,M,D$ satisfy the conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#altord:cb:reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"altord:cb:reduction\">19</a>. Let $\\nu:Y'\\to Y$ be a smooth projective log resolution, and write $$K_{Y'}+B^{\\mathrm{cr}}=\\nu^*(K_Y+B).$$ Set $B'=(B^{\\mathrm{cr}})^+$, $M'=\\nu^*M$, and $D'=K_{Y'}+B'+M'$. Then $B'$ is effective, $(Y',B')$ is klt, $M'$ is nef with the same Hodge realization after pullback, and $$D'=\\nu^*D+(B^{\\mathrm{cr}})^-.$$ The additional divisor is effective and $\\nu$-exceptional. Consequently $\\kappa(Y',D')=\\kappa(Y,D)$, and $D'$ is big on the geometric generic fibre over $Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-altord-a-log-general-type",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u:V\\to T$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibres, where $V$ is normal projective and $T$ is smooth projective. Let $\\Delta\\geq0$ be a $\\mathbf Q$-divisor such that $(V,\\Delta)$ is log canonical. If the log canonical divisor of the geometric generic fibre $G$ is big and $\\kappa(T)\\geq0$, then $$\\kappa(V,K_V+\\Delta)\\geq \\dim G+\\kappa(T).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-altord-a-log-general-type",
          "exact_quote": "Let $u:V\\to T$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibres, where $V$ is normal projective and $T$ is smooth projective.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p1logalt-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety and $D$ a reduced simple normal crossing divisor. Let $\\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on $P\\setminus D$ whose highest nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line, and let $L\\in\\operatorname{Pic}(P)\\otimes\\mathbf Q$ be its rational parabolic extension. There are a projective birational morphism $\\pi:\\widetilde P\\to P$, with $\\widetilde P$ smooth, a smooth projective variety $Z$, a surjective morphism $h:\\widetilde P\\to Z$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational divisor $A$ on $Z$ such that $$\\pi^*L\\sim_{\\mathbf Q}h^*A,\n \\qquad K_Z+aA\\ \\text{is big for every sufficiently large rational }a.$$ Here a divisor on a point is big. Further smooth birational modifications of the source are permitted by replacing $h$ with its composition with the modification.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p1logalt-extensions",
        "exact_quote": "The rational extension has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *It is nef and is compatible with positive tensor powers and pullback by morphisms of smooth projective log pairs whose interiors map to the original interior.*\n\n2.  *In the unipotent case, on each connected boundary stratum the local limiting mixed Hodge structures determine a unique weight-graded piece containing the top Hodge line. These pieces glue to a polarizable pure variation with rank-one top filtration, whose extended top line on the closure of the stratum is the restriction of $L$.*\n\n3.  *The preceding restriction statement remains valid after an adapted cover in the quasi-unipotent case. In particular, numerical dimensions of restrictions may be computed on a smooth adapted cover of the stratum.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p1logalt-rank",
        "exact_quote": "The numerical dimension of the rational top Hodge line equals the generic rank of its line period map: $$\\nu(L)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}(d\\ell)_{\\mathrm{gen}}.$$ In the unipotent case, if $d=\\dim P$, $\\omega$ is a smooth Kähler form, and $\\alpha$ is the first Chern form of the Hodge metric on $L|_{P^\\circ}$, then, for $0\\le k\\le d$, $$\\label{p1logalt:chern}\n \\int_{P^\\circ}\\alpha^k\\wedge\\omega^{d-k}\n =c_1(L)^k\\cdot[\\omega]^{d-k}.$$ The integrals are absolutely convergent.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-033.4"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on $U$ whose highest nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line $L^\\circ$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p4int-transverse-loop",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U=\\Delta\\times\\Delta^{d-1}$, with coordinates $(z,s)$, and let $U^*=\\Delta^*\\times\\Delta^{d-1}$. Suppose that a holomorphic map $\\ell:\\widetilde{U^*}\\to\\mathbb P(V)$ is equivariant for a linear transformation $T\\in\\operatorname{GL}(V)$ under one positive turn around $z=0$, and that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}(d\\ell)\\leq r$. Suppose that a holomorphic map $q:U\\to\\mathbb C^r$ has tangential differential of rank $r$ at a point of $z=0$, and that on $U^*$ its lift satisfies $\\ker(d\\ell)\\subseteq\\ker(dq)$. Then $T$ fixes every line in the image of $\\ell$ on a nonempty open subset. If $\\ell$ is the restriction of a line period map on a connected universal cover, $T$ fixes that entire line image. This includes $r=0$, when $q$ is the map to a point.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Kodaira dimension** ([source](#sec:introduction)): More precisely, the Kodaira dimension $\kappa(X)$ is the maximum dimension of the images of the complete linear systems $\|mK_X\|$, for positive integers $m$; it is $-\infty$ if every such system is empty.
- **algebraic fibre space** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An algebraic fibre space is a surjective projective morphism with connected fibres.
- **rational linear equivalence** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We write $A\sim_{\mathbf Q}B$ when a positive integral multiple of $A-B$ is principal.
- **Hodge line** ([source](#sec:rational)): We call this piece the *Hodge line*.
- **Hodge-generic point** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We call a lifted point *Hodge-generic* if it lies outside every proper Hodge locus of a rational flat tensor in tensor constructions on the variation and its dual.
- **minimal for its highest line** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A variation is *minimal for its highest line* if the generic fibre is the smallest rational Hodge substructure containing that line.
- **numerical dimension** ([source](#sec:limits)): For a nef line $J$ on a smooth projective $t$-fold we use $$\nu(J)=\max\{a\in\{0,\ldots,t\}:c_1(J)^aH^{t-a}>0\},$$ where $H$ is ample.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-subadditivity | Kodaira dimension of the source | ≥ | $\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z)$ | F geometric generic fibre, Z base |
| q-numerical-dimension-rank | Numerical dimension of the extended Hodge line | = | $r$ | r generic rank of the highest-line differential |
| q-rank-loss | Numerical dimension of the restriction to a boundary or ramification divisor | < | $r$ | E component of D_N or fixed by a nonidentity element |
| q-rank-positive | Numerical dimension of the highest line | > | $0$ | r equals the numerical dimension of J |
| q-interpolation-weight | Interpolation weight | = | $\frac{1-v}{j-v}$ | rational v strictly between zero and one, integer j larger than one |
| q-tensor-exponent | Tensor exponent making the line monodromy faithful | = | $\operatorname{lcm}\{q\geq1:\varphi(q)\leq R\}$ | R rank of the retained subvariation |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-minimal** [claim](#stmt-altord-h-minimal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-h-minimal): Intersect rational Hodge substructures containing the line, then spread by analytic continuation from a Hodge-generic point. -> unique smallest rational subvariation containing the line
- **step-normality** [claim](#stmt-altord-p-normality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-p-normality): Normal subgroups of semisimple monodromy are products of simple factors and are preserved by the Mumford-Tate group, so invariants are a subrepresentation. -> trivial connected rational normal subgroup
- **step-inertia** [claim](#stmt-altord-p-inertia) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-p-inertia): Show the automorphism centralizes monodromy using the normality lemma, then apply the fixed-part theorem. -> automorphism preserves the whole Hodge filtration
- **step-numerical-rank** [claim](#stmt-altord-h-numerical-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-h-numerical-rank): Restrict to general complete intersections and use bigness criteria from the extension inputs. -> numerical dimension equals generic rank
- **step-boundary-line** [claim](#stmt-altord-h-boundary-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-h-boundary-line): Use the Deligne bigrading of the limiting mixed Hodge structure and hard Lefschetz to identify a primitive limiting line. -> bound on boundary numerical dimension
- **step-rank-principle** [claim](#stmt-altord-h-rank-principle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-h-rank-principle): Constant rank theorem and a contraction mapping argument in adapted coordinates. -> nearby image lies in the limiting image
- **step-nilpotent-limit** [claim](#stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit): Expand the exponential and estimate remainders on a sector. -> tangential convergence into the projective kernel
- **step-rankloss** [claim](#stmt-thm-rankloss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-rankloss): Argue by contradiction in the nilpotent and finite inertia cases using the rank principle, normality and inertia lemmas. -> strict rank drop on boundary and ramification divisors
- **step-volume** [claim](#stmt-altord-e-volume-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-e-volume-lemma): Asymptotic Riemann-Roch with a divisor exact sequence removing the components one at a time. -> bigness of the adjoint class for large multiples
- **step-exceptional-pullback** [claim](#stmt-altord-e-exceptional-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-e-exceptional-pullback): Factor through the normalization and use normality for exceptional divisors. -> bigness unchanged by exceptional effective twist
- **step-adjoint-positivity** [claim](#stmt-altord-p-observation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-e-exceptional-pullback-2): Construct the period image and its finite quotient, descend the line, then remove boundary and ramification using rank loss and the volume lemma. -> descended nef line with big adjoint class
- **step-root-cover** [claim](#stmt-altord-cb-root-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-cb-root-cover): Kummer extension of the function field, ramification control, and invariance of Iitaka dimension under finite pullback. -> resolution of Kodaira dimension zero with a unique canonical line
- **step-reduction** [claim](#stmt-altord-cb-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-cb-reduction): Relative Iitaka fibration, connected-fibre factorization and the canonical bundle formula. -> factorization with canonical bundle data
- **step-comparison** [claim](#stmt-altord-cb-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-cb-comparison): Compare sections via the formula and residual direct image, then use flat base change to the geometric generic fibre. -> Kodaira dimension inequality and generic bigness
- **step-high-model** [claim](#stmt-altord-cb-high-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-cb-high-model): Crepant pullback to a log resolution and exceptional twist argument. -> data preserved on higher models
- **step-period-fibre** [claim](#stmt-altord-a-period-fibre) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-a-period-fibre): Stein factorization, cotangent sequence on fibres, then apply the log-general-type subadditivity theorem. -> nonnegative log Kodaira dimension of the period fibre
- **step-effectivity** [claim](#stmt-altord-a-effectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-a-effectivity): Flatten the fibration, apply twisted weak positivity to the direct image, and push forward the resulting section. -> effective representative of the twisted relative class
- **step-ordinary-complex** [claim](#stmt-altord-ordinary-subadditivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-a-effectivity-2): Interpolate the big adjoint class between an effective divisor and a klt log canonical class, then apply the log-general-type theorem. -> subadditivity over the complex numbers
- **step-field-extension** [claim](#stmt-altord-a-field-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-altord-a-field-extension): Flat base change of pluricanonical spaces. -> Kodaira dimension unchanged by ground field extension
- **step-tensor-replacement** [claim](#stmt-p1logalt-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logalt-kernel): Isotypic decomposition, compactness of the stabilizer and a uniform tensor exponent. -> variation faithful on its moving line
- **step-descent** [claim](#stmt-p1logalt-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logalt-descent): Algebraic period image normalized in the relative algebraic closure, then monodromy killed along fibres by the tensor replacement. -> descended variation on a smooth projective base
- **step-transverse-loop** [claim](#stmt-p4int-transverse-loop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4int-transverse-loop): Submersion coordinates and a loop argument. -> monodromy fixes the moving line
- **step-boundary-drop** [claim](#stmt-p1logalt-boundary-drop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logalt-boundary-drop): Untwist by a root cover, project the limiting line to a fixed eigenspace quotient, then apply the transverse-loop lemma and faithfulness. -> strict boundary rank drop
- **step-boundary-subtraction** [claim](#stmt-p1logalt-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logalt-volume): Kodaira lemma and a uniform section estimate comparing polynomial degrees in the parameter. -> bigness of the adjoint class for large parameter
- **step-integral-positivity** [claim](#stmt-p1logalt-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logalt-volume-2): Combine tensor replacement, descent, log general type of the complement, boundary drop and boundary subtraction. -> adjoint positivity for the rational parabolic extension
- **step-chern-forms** [claim](#stmt-p1varhd-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1varhd-curvature): Product Poincare bound for the curvature, logarithmic growth of the metric, cutoff and integration by parts. -> Chern intersection formula with closed test forms
- **step-thom-boundary** [claim](#stmt-p1varhd-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1varhd-boundary): Limiting Hodge norm, Thom form concentration with crossing estimates, then fixed quotient and transverse loops. -> strict boundary rank drop by an analytic route

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-ordinary-subadditivity**: For a surjective projective morphism with connected fibres between smooth connected projective varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, the Kodaira dimension of the source is at least the sum of the Kodaira dimensions of the geometric generic fibre and of the base.
- **inv-rank-loss**: On every boundary component with nonzero monodromy logarithm, and on every component fixed pointwise by a nonidentity element of the effective finite group, the numerical dimension of the restricted extended highest Hodge line is strictly smaller than the numerical dimension of the line itself.
- **inv-numerical-rank**: For the extended Hodge line on a smooth projective compactification with unipotent boundary monodromy, the numerical dimension equals the generic differential rank of the highest-line map.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-hypothesis-violation**: Apply the subadditivity inequality to a morphism that is not projective, not surjective, lacks connected fibres, has singular varieties, or lives over a field not algebraically closed of characteristic zero. -> The theorem gives no conclusion outside its stated setting.
- **anti-immersivity-as-conclusion**: Read generic immersivity of the full period map as something the rank-loss result proves, or identify the full-period rank with the rank of the highest line. -> The two ranks are different objects and need not agree.
- **anti-abundance-input**: Assume abundance or semiampleness of the moduli divisor as an input to the canonical bundle step. -> The argument does not supply such an assertion, so conclusions relying on it are unsupported.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and inequality theorems of algebraic geometry and Hodge theory over arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. They rely on period-image algebraicity, canonical bundle formulas and asymptotic section counts, and the paper gives no finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs that could be run and cross-checked.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a non-constructive theorem in algebraic geometry. It gives no finite procedure that a program could execute. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns simulation, dynamics or interactive systems; the objects are projective varieties and variations of Hodge structure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an inequality between Kodaira dimensions. It does not define a procedure that a checker or tool could run. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proofs use existence arguments such as Stein factorization, resolution of singularities and asymptotic Riemann–Roch. They give no algorithm with explicit steps or cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced. The objects are abstract varieties, line bundles and local systems. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source has no arithmetic kernel, parallel structure or cost model that could be mapped to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security notion appears. The characteristic-zero algebraic geometry has no cryptographic content. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning, data or statistical model is involved. Period maps are holomorphic maps between complex analytic spaces. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective, constraint set or optimisation problem. Bigness and numerical dimension are qualitative invariants of divisors. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source has no notion of nodes, communication or consensus. A finite group acting on a variety is a symmetry, not a distributed system. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The contribution is purely theoretical: it settles ordinary Iitaka subadditivity in characteristic zero. It yields no engineering artefact, so nothing is claimed beyond mathematics. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-field-and-morphism-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The main inequality is proved only for a surjective projective morphism with connected fibres between smooth connected projective varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequality over fields that are not algebraically closed or not of characteristic zero', 'Applying it to non-projective or non-surjective morphisms, or to morphisms without connected fibres or between singular varieties']
- **limit-minus-infinity-convention** (edge_case): The inequality is read with a convention for the value minus infinity and for a point, so the statement is meaningful when the fibre or base has no pluricanonical forms. Blocked use: ['Treating minus infinity as an ordinary number in sums of Kodaira dimensions']
- **limit-immersivity-is-hypothesis** (hypothesis): Generic immersivity is a hypothesis on the full period map. The rank-loss conclusion concerns the highest line only, and the two ranks are not identified. Blocked use: ['Reading immersivity of the full period map as a conclusion', 'Identifying the full-period rank with the numerical dimension of the highest line']
- **limit-rank-loss-setting** (formal_scope): The strict rank drop is proved only for the specified data: a variation minimal for its highest line with unipotent boundary monodromy, a finite group action, and a base birational to the actual period image. Blocked use: ['Applying the rank drop to a variation that is not minimal for its highest line', 'Replacing the actual period image by an arbitrary finite extension of its function field']
- **limit-no-abundance-input** (nonconstructive): No abundance assertion for the moduli divisor is part of the input, so the argument gives no semiampleness or abundance conclusion for it. Blocked use: ['Assuming abundance or semiampleness of the moduli divisor as an available input']
- **limit-nonconstructive-proof** (nonconstructive): The proof relies on existence arguments such as resolutions, alterations and descent, so no finite procedure computing the objects involved is given. Blocked use: ['Treating the proof as an algorithm that computes Kodaira dimensions or period images']
- **limit-adjoint-positivity-conditions** (hypothesis): Adjoint positivity requires that, after birational modification, an alteration exists with unipotent monodromy along a normal crossing boundary and with the class equal to a positive multiple of the extended highest line. Blocked use: ['Invoking adjoint positivity for a class not tied to the whole highest Hodge line']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the ordinary Iitaka subadditivity conjecture for surjective projective morphisms with connected fibers between smooth connected projective varieties over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. If <i>F</i> is the geometric generic fiber of $`f:X\to Z`$, then $`\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z)`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

|---|---|---|---|---|

| [stmt-altord-p-observation](#stmt-altord-p-observation) | theorem | `altord:p:observation` | 2 | Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety, let $U\subset Y$ be a dense Zariski open subset, and let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation of Hodge structure on |
| [stmt-altord-h-minimal](#stmt-altord-h-minimal) | lemma | `altord:h:minimal` | 3 | There is a unique smallest rational subvariation $V^{\min}\subset V_{\mathbf Q}$ whose Hodge filtration contains $F^mV_{\mathcal O}$. |
| [stmt-altord-p-normality](#stmt-altord-p-normality) | lemma | `altord:p:normality` | 4 | Let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation on a smooth connected complex algebraic variety, minimal for its deepest Hodge line. |
| [stmt-altord-p-inertia](#stmt-altord-p-inertia) | lemma | `altord:p:inertia` | 5 | Let $U$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety with a rationally polarized integral pure variation $\mathbb V$ minimal for its deepest Hodge line. |

| [stmt-altord-h-boundary-line](#stmt-altord-h-boundary-line) | proposition | `altord:h:boundary-line` | 8 | On a transverse coordinate chart $(u,v)$ about a general point of $E$, with $E=(u=0)$, write a generator of the lifted Hodge line as |
| [stmt-altord-h-rank-principle](#stmt-altord-h-rank-principle) | lemma | `altord:h:rank-principle` | 9 | Let $B\subset\mathbf C^n$ be a polydisc and let $f:S_\epsilon\times B\to M$ be a holomorphic map to a complex manifold. |
| [stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit](#stmt-altord-h-nilpotent-limit) | lemma | `altord:h:nilpotent-limit` | 10 | Let $N$ be a nilpotent endomorphism of a finite-dimensional complex vector space, and let $a(u,v)$ be holomorphic on $\Delta\times B$. |
| [stmt-altord-h-scaling](#stmt-altord-h-scaling) | remark | `altord:h:scaling` | - | Remark 11*. All numerical-rank statements are unchanged on replacing a Hodge line by a positive rational multiple of its class in $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(T)\otimes\mathbf Q$. |
| [stmt-thm-rankloss](#stmt-thm-rankloss) | theorem | `thm:rankloss` | 12 | For the data just specified, put $r=\nu(J)$. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<span id="altord:section" label="altord:section"></span>

Let $f:X\to Z$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibres between smooth projective varieties. The pluricanonical forms on $X$ measure its birational complexity. Iitaka’s subadditivity problem asks whether the forms on the base and on the geometric generic fibre always contribute additively to that complexity. More precisely, the Kodaira dimension $\kappa(X)$ is the maximum dimension of the images of the complete linear systems $|mK_X|$, for positive integers $m$; it is $-\infty$ if every such system is empty. If $F$ is the geometric generic fibre of $f$, the conjecture $C_{n,m}$ asks for $$\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z),
                 \qquad n=\dim X,\quad m=\dim Z.$$ We use $(-\infty)+a=-\infty$ and $\kappa(\mathrm{point})=0$.

The problem belongs to Iitaka’s theory of dimensions of divisors (Iitaka 1971) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[IitakaOriginal](#ref-IitakaOriginal)]. Its development has repeatedly connected the growth of pluricanonical systems with positivity on the base. Viehweg’s weak-positivity method gives addition when the base is of general type (Viehweg 1983) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ViehwegAdditivity](#ref-ViehwegAdditivity)]. Kawamata proved addition over curves and when the general fibre has a good minimal model (Kawamata 1982, 1985) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KawamataCurves](#ref-KawamataCurves), [KawamataKodaira](#ref-KawamataKodaira)]; Kollár established the case of general-type fibres (Kollár 1987) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kollar87](#ref-Kollar87)]. The log-general-type-fibre theorem of Kovács–Patakfalvi and its formulation by Hashizume provide the addition theorem used below (Kovács and Patakfalvi 2017; Hashizume 2020) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KovacsPatakfalvi](#ref-KovacsPatakfalvi), [Hashizume](#ref-Hashizume)].

Birkar proved the conjecture in total dimension at most six (Birkar 2009) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BirkarIitakaSix](#ref-BirkarIitakaSix)]. Using canonical-bundle and nonvanishing methods, Chang extended this to total dimension at most seven under the additional assumption $\kappa(X)\geq0$ (Chang 2025, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ChangNonvanishing](#ref-ChangNonvanishing)]. Other advances include Cao–Păun’s theorem for projective klt pairs over abelian varieties, Hacon–Popa–Schnell’s result for bases of maximal Albanese dimension, and Cao’s theorem for projective klt pairs over surfaces (Cao and Păun 2017; Hacon, Popa, and Schnell 2018; Cao 2018) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CaoPaunAbelian](#ref-CaoPaunAbelian), [HaconPopaSchnellAbelian](#ref-HaconPopaSchnellAbelian), [CaoSurfaces](#ref-CaoSurfaces)]. These results show how information about the base and positivity of direct images can control the total pluricanonical system. Tsuji’s preprint states ordinary subadditivity in Theorem 1.13, while Maehara’s preprint states a broader variation inequality in Section 7, Theorem 8 (Tsuji 2010; Maehara 2010) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[TsujiDirectImages](#ref-TsujiDirectImages), [MaeharaIitaka](#ref-MaeharaIitaka)]. Those preprint claims are not inputs to our proof. The argument here is a projective proof through a Hodge line and a canonical bundle formula.

<a id="stmt-altord-ordinary-subadditivity"></a>
<div id="altord:ordinary-subadditivity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Ordinary Iitaka subadditivity). *<span id="thm:ordinary" label="thm:ordinary"></span>Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a surjective projective morphism with connected fibres between smooth connected projective $k$-varieties. If $F$ is its geometric generic fibre, then $$\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Z).$$*

</div>

Thus the ordinary Iitaka conjecture has a positive answer in this setting. The main step in the proof is a positivity statement for the entire highest Hodge line of a rationally polarized variation. We first describe that statement, then explain how the canonical bundle formula brings it into the subadditivity problem.

Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety and $U$ a dense open subset. A rationally polarized integral pure variation $\mathbb V$ on $U$ consists of a rational local system with a monodromy-invariant lattice, a Hodge filtration of fixed weight, and a rational flat polarization. Suppose its highest nonzero filtration step $F^p\mathbb V_{\mathcal O}$ is a line, so $F^{p+1}=0$ and $\operatorname{rank}F^p=1$. On a simply connected open set, a flat marking identifies this line with a holomorphic map to the projective space of a fixed reference fibre. We call this the *highest-line map*. The *full period map* records every step of the Hodge filtration.

The highest line has a rational parabolic extension across a smooth normal-crossing compactification. This is the extension whose pullback, after a finite level cover and resolution making local monodromy unipotent, is the Deligne–Schmid highest line. The rational weights retain the finite parts of the original local monodromies.

<a id="stmt-altord-p-observation"></a>
<div id="altord:p:observation" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Projective adjoint positivity). *<span id="thm:adjoint" label="thm:adjoint"></span>Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety, let $U\subset Y$ be a dense Zariski open subset, and let $\mathbb V$ be a rationally polarized integral pure variation of Hodge structure on $U$ whose entire highest nonzero Hodge filtration step is a line. Let $M\in\operatorname{Pic}(Y)\otimes\mathbf Q$. Assume that, after a smooth projective birational modification of $Y$ if necessary, there are a smooth projective alteration $\tau:\widehat Y\to Y$ and a rational number $c>0$ such that the pulled-back variation has unipotent monodromy along a simple-normal-crossing boundary and $$\tau^*M\sim_{\mathbf Q}cJ_{\widehat Y},$$ where $J_{\widehat Y}$ is its extended highest line. After further smooth projective birational modification, there are a surjective morphism $p:Y\to S$ with connected fibres onto a smooth projective variety and a nef rational line $L$ on $S$ such that $$M\sim_{\mathbf Q}p^*L,\qquad
 K_S+jL\text{ is big for all sufficiently large integers }j.$$ If $S$ is a point, the conclusion is $M\sim_{\mathbf Q}0$.*

</div>

The two period maps have separate jobs. The full period image provides the projective base on which an adjoint divisor can be big. The numerical dimension of $L$ is measured by the highest-line map, whose rank can be smaller. The argument must therefore remove the boundary using the rank of the line, without identifying it with the dimension of the full period image.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The boundary obstruction and its resolution

The algebraicity theorem of Bakker–Brunebarbe–Tsimerman constructs the full period image as an algebraic variety (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman](#ref-BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman)]. At a neat level, Brunebarbe–Cadorel’s theorem gives logarithmic general type when the full period map is generically immersive (Brunebarbe and Cadorel 2020, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BrunebarbeCadorel](#ref-BrunebarbeCadorel)]. A finite quotient returns to the original level. This introduces a second divisor to be removed: besides the boundary with nonzero monodromy logarithm, there is divisorial ramification of the finite quotient. Ordinary adjoint positivity requires control of both.

We first retain the smallest rational subvariation containing the highest line. This is the pure transcendental-part construction of Bakker–Filipazzi–Mauri–Tsimerman (Bakker et al. 2025, Definition 2.9) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)].
