On the punctured chart it factors through the moving highest-line map, since the exponential of $N$ disappears in $C_N$. Its boundary differential rank is at least the rank $q_i$ of the primitive graded line.

Suppose $\nu(A|_{D_i})\geq r$. By <a href="#p1varhd:restrictionrank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1varhd:restrictionrank">[p1varhd:restrictionrank]</a>, the projected map has at least $r$ independent tangential differentials at a general boundary point. Choose $r$ affine components $g_1,\ldots,g_r$ with that property. Their differentials are independent along the boundary at that point, and the map $g=(g_1,\ldots,g_r)$ extends holomorphically over the original chart. On the punctured chart it factors through the highest-line map. Lemma <a href="#p1varhd:curvature" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1varhd:curvature">37</a> gives the latter rank at most $r$. The hypotheses of the transverse-loop criterion (Lemma <a href="#p4int:transverse-loop" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p4int:transverse-loop">33</a>) are therefore satisfied in the original transverse coordinate, before the finite root cover. It follows that the original transverse monodromy fixes the moving line on the connected universal cover. This contradicts faithfulness and its nonidentity. When $r=0$, use the same lemma with an empty list of functions; when $r>n-1$, the supposed inequality $\nu(A|_{D_i})\geq r$ is impossible on dimensional grounds. This proves Equation <a href="#p1varhd:strictdrop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1varhd:strictdrop">[p1varhd:strictdrop]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
#### Connection with the integral projective argument

The hypotheses required for the preceding calculation arise from the integral tensor replacement and full-period descent in Lemmas <a href="#p1logalt:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:kernel">31</a> and <a href="#p1logalt:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:descent">32</a>. On the resulting projective base, Lemma <a href="#p1varhd:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1varhd:boundary">38</a> gives the same strict inequality as Lemma <a href="#p1logalt:boundary-drop" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:boundary-drop">34</a>, with the boundary intersection justified directly by Thom forms. The boundary subtraction estimate of Lemma <a href="#p1logalt:volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:volume">35</a> then applies to the union $J$ of the divisors with nonidentity transverse monodromy. The full-period map supplies the bigness of $K_R+J$ as in the proof of Theorem <a href="#p1logalt:positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:positivity">28</a>; the highest-line rank supplies the strict decrease in the degree of the restriction polynomials. Thus the two different period ranks retain their separate roles in adjoint positivity.

<a id="stmt-p4int-restricted-tensor"></a>
<div id="p4int:restricted-tensor" class="remark">

*Remark 39* (Tensor replacement on a restricted locus). The same local results also give the curve-constancy part of the highest-line properties used in such comparisons. On a smooth complete curve, degree zero gives zero integral of the semipositive Hodge curvature by Equation <a href="#p1varhd:chern" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p1varhd:chern">[p1varhd:chern]</a>, after an adapted cover if necessary. The curvature is therefore zero, and the highest line is projectively constant in a flat reference. If the highest line is projectively constant on a connected smooth algebraic locus, apply Lemma <a href="#p1logalt:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logalt:kernel">31</a> to the restricted variation itself. Use its own monodromy image and isotypic decomposition: restricting an ambient replacement need not preserve faithfulness, because the restricted line image may have a larger projective stabilizer. Every monodromy element of the replacement fixes its constant highest line, so faithfulness makes that monodromy trivial. Since the new line is a positive tensor power of the original line, the original highest-line character on that locus has finite image. The tensor exponent here is allowed to depend on the restricted variation.

</div>

<a id="stmt-p1varhd-further-models"></a>
<div id="p1varhd:further-models" class="remark">

*Remark 40*. The adjoint conclusion is preserved under further smooth projective birational modification $\pi:R'\to R$, together with a compatible modification of the source. The parabolic pullback property preserves the line-bundle identity and nefness, while $$K_{R'}+b\pi^*A
   =\pi^*(K_R+bA)+E_\pi,\qquad E_\pi\geq0,$$ preserves bigness. The calculations here concern the whole rank-one highest step of a polarizable integral pure variation on a projective pair; they assert no equality between highest-line rank, full-period rank, and whole-fiber birational variation.

</div>
